I don't know if I'd call it anxious, exactly. It's more just... I guess my mind just goes straight back to the spreadsheets and the forecasts. It's not like a panic or anything terrible, I promise. I don't want to sound like I can't handle my job. I'm actually very good at it, usually. I'm sure you see people with much more stressful situations all the time, don't you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been a little while now. I'd say... maybe about three years? It started around the time our company had a big merger and they reorganized the Caribbean-market division I work in. \n\nI know three years sounds like a long time to let this go on, and I really should have figured out how to handle it better by now. I just kept thinking it would settle down once the new structure became routine, you know? I didn't want to make a fuss or bother anyone about it. I hope it's okay that I waited so long to come in... I just didn't want to seem like I couldn't adapt to the changes. I just didn't want to seem like I couldn't adapt to the changes. Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I try really hard not to be one of those people who just... worries about everything. Work takes up so much of my mental energy with all the market reports and analysis, so I just try to keep everything else running as smoothly as possible. 

I wouldn't say I'm a worrier, really. I just try to handle whatever comes up at home without making a fuss or burdening anyone else with my stress. I hope that's okay to say? I just... Um... no, not really. I've never really been a night owl, to be honest. I mean, I know some people really thrive late at night, but I just... I don't think my body wants to do that. 

I really prefer being up early and getting a head start on the day. It just feels more responsible, you know? Like I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing. If I stayed up late and slept in, I'd just feel so guilty, like I was wasting the day or letting people down. I wouldn't want my husband or kids to think I'm lazy. I just didn't want to seem like I couldn't adapt to the changes. Is it pretty common for people to take a while to seek help for things like this?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired or fatigued during the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do feel a little tired sometimes during the day. I still get all my work done and meet my deadlines. I might just need an extra cup of coffee in the afternoon, but that's perfectly normal for an office job, don't you think? I really try not to let it affect my performance. I just... I don't want to sound like I'm complaining about being a little sleepy when there are people with real problems. Does that sound like a typical amount of tiredness to you?\n\nDoctor: Has the sleep trouble been constant since the company merger, or have there been periods where it got better or worse?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's pretty much been... constant, I guess. Like I mentioned, it's been going on for about three years, ever since the merger and the whole reorganization of the Caribbean-market division. \n\nI mean, maybe there were a few nights here and there where I slept a tiny bit better, but overall... yeah, it's just been this steady thing since then. I really hope I don't sound like I'm whining about it. I know it's my own fault for not just adjusting better to the changes. I just kept thinking I'd get the hang of it and my sleep would go back to normal. That makes sense, right? I mean, I try really hard not to be one of those people who just... worries about everything. I just try to handle whatever comes up at home without making a fuss or burdening anyone else with my stress. I hope that's okay to say? I just... I don't think there's a specific time where I suddenly feel super alert or anything. Most people probably feel a little sluggish all day when they aren't sleeping, right? That's pretty standard?\n\nDoctor: Do you often go to bed and wake up at the same times every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I'd say I'm pretty consistent. I really try to keep a strict schedule, you know? I usually go to bed around eleven and I'm up by six, even on Saturdays and Sundays. \n\nI just... I feel like it's important to maintain a routine so I can be ready for the week ahead and not let things slide. I read somewhere that doctors recommend that, so I've really been trying my best to stick to it. I hope that's the right thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling like you're sleeping at the wrong times of the day or night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I really do try to stick to that eleven to six schedule, so I'm not really sleeping during the day at all. \n\nI guess sometimes I might feel a little heavy-eyed in the afternoon, like after lunch, but I never actually fall asleep at my desk or anything. I just power through it with some water or a quick walk around the office. I'd never want anyone to think I'm slacking off. It's totally normal to get a little sleepy in the afternoon, right? I think a lot of people feel that way, so I just try to handle it as best I can. I hope I'm doing the right thing by just pushing through?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep pattern would change if you were not working?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I don't think it would change much. I feel like if I didn't have that structure, I might just sort of fade into the background, you know? I want to be useful, especially for my family. I wouldn't want to become a burden or just lay around all day. That would be terrible, right? So I'd still make sure I'm up at six, getting things done around the house or running errands. It's better to stay busy and productive, don't you think? I'm pretty sure that's the healthiest way to handle things, even if you're not working.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're more of a night owl, or that your body wants to stay up later and sleep in later than your current schedule allows?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I've never really been a night owl, to be honest. I mean, I know some people really thrive late at night, but I just... I don't think my body wants to do that. \n\nI really prefer being up early and getting a head start on the day. It just feels more responsible, you know? Like I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing. If I stayed up late and slept in, I'd just feel so guilty, like I was wasting the day or letting people down. I wouldn't want my husband or kids to think I'm lazy. It's much better to just stick to the schedule and be dependable, right? I think that's what a good mother and wife should do, anyway. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, a vacation sounds lovely, but... I probably wouldn't even make it to 6 am, to be honest. 

I... I guess I should just admit this. I didn't want to say it earlier because I didn't want to sound like I'm complaining or that I can't handle my job, but... that's actually a big part of why I came in to see you. I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. Even if I didn't have anywhere to be, I'd probably just lie awake in the dark replaying client financial models in my head. 

Lately, I've been waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning, and my stomach is just... so tight. I can't go back to sleep, so I just end up going to work feeling like I never actually rested at all. I just want to be thorough with my accounts and make sure I'm doing my best for everyone, you know? But waking up at 3 am feeling sick and exhausted like that... that's probably not normal, is it? I hope it's okay that I'm finally bringing it up.

I really try not to let it affect my performance. I just... I don't want to sound like I'm complaining about being a little sleepy when there are people with real problems. Does that sound like a typical amount of tiredness to you?\n\nDoctor: Has the sleep trouble been constant since the company merger, or have there been periods where it got better or worse?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's pretty much been... constant, I guess. Like I mentioned, it's been going on for about three years, ever since the merger and the whole reorganization of the Caribbean-market division. \n\nI mean, maybe there were a few nights here and there where I slept a tiny bit better, but overall... yeah, it's just been this steady thing since then. I really hope I don't sound like I'm whining about it. I know it's my own fault for not just adjusting better to the changes. I just kept thinking I'd get the hang of it and my sleep would go back to normal. That makes sense, right? Wanting to handle it on my own before bothering a doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I try really hard not to be one of those people who just... worries about everything. Work takes up so much of my mental energy with all the market reports and analysis, so I just try to keep everything else running as smoothly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't say I'm a worrier, really. I just try to handle whatever comes up at home without making a fuss or burdening anyone else with my stress. I hope that's okay to say? I just... I try to stay focused on what I can control so I'm not a bother to my family. That's a pretty normal way to handle things, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty concentrating at work due to lack of sleep?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I have noticed a little bit of a difference lately. I mean, I really try to push through it, I promise. But... you know those spreadsheets I work on? They used to be just second nature to me. Now, it's like... I don't know, they just take me hours sometimes. \n\nI find myself losing the thread of what I'm doing, and I end up rereading the same assumptions over and over again. And then I just... I get so worried I've missed something obvious right before a presentation. I really hate admitting that, because I want to be doing a good job. I mean, everyone gets a little distracted when they're tired, right? It's not like I'm completely incompetent or anything, I just... I have to double-check things more than I used to. That's a pretty normal reaction to not sleeping well, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling most alert or awake at a specific time of day, regardless of how much sleep you got the night before?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think there's a specific time where I suddenly feel super alert or anything. It's just sort of... well, a steady kind of tired all day. \n\nI mean, I try to have my coffee and get myself going in the mornings so I'm ready for the day, but I wouldn't say I have a burst of energy at any point. I just kind of keep pushing through, you know? I hope that's an okay answer. I just haven't really noticed a specific time where I feel wide awake. Most people probably feel a little sluggish all day when they aren't sleeping, right? That's pretty standard?\n\nDoctor: Do you often go to bed and wake up at the same times every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I'd say I'm pretty consistent. I really try to keep a strict schedule, you know? I usually go to bed around eleven and I'm up by six, even on Saturdays and Sundays. \n\nI just... I feel like it's important to maintain a routine so I can be ready for the week ahead and not let things slide. I don't want my family to have to deal with me sleeping in and messing up our weekend plans. I mean, it's good practice to keep a regular sleep schedule, isn't it? I read somewhere that doctors recommend that, so I've really been trying my best to stick to it. I hope that's the right thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling like you're sleeping at the wrong times of the day or night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I really do try to stick to that eleven to six schedule, so I'm not really sleeping during the day at all. \n\nI guess sometimes I might feel a little heavy-eyed in the afternoon, like after lunch, but I never actually fall asleep at my desk or anything. I just power through it with some water or a quick walk around the office. I'd never want anyone to think I'm slacking off. It's totally normal to get a little sleepy in the afternoon, right? I think a lot of people feel that way, so I just try to handle it as best I can. I hope I'm doing the right thing by just pushing through?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep pattern would change if you were not working?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I don't think it would change much. I mean, even if I didn't have the office to go to, I'd still want to keep my routine. \n\nI just... I feel like if I didn't have that structure, I might just sort of fade into the background, you know? I want to be useful, especially for my family. I wouldn't want to become a burden or just lay around all day. That would be terrible, right? So I'd still make sure I'm up at six, getting things done around the house or running errands. It's better to stay busy and productive, don't you think? I'm pretty sure that's the healthiest way to handle things, even if you're not working.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're more of a night owl, or that your body wants to stay up later and sleep in later than your current schedule allows?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I've never really been a night owl, to be honest. I mean, I know some people really thrive late at night, but I just... I don't think my body wants to do that. \n\nI really prefer being up early and getting a head start on the day. It just feels more responsible, you know? Like I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing. If I stayed up late and slept in, I'd just feel so guilty, like I was wasting the day or letting people down. I wouldn't want my husband or kids to think I'm lazy. It's much better to just stick to the schedule and be dependable, right? I think that's what a good mother and wife should do, anyway. I hope that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would naturally wake up at the same time if you didn't have to go to work?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think I would. I mean, even on weekends or when I have a day off, my eyes just sort of pop open right around six anyway. \n\nIt's like my body is just... trained to do it, I guess. I wouldn't want to sleep in and waste the morning. There's always so much to do—laundry, getting breakfast ready, just making sure the house is in order before everyone else gets going. I feel like if I slept in, I'd just be starting the day off on the wrong foot, you know? It's better to be up and ready to go. That's a good habit to have, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing my part.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still wake up at 6 am if you were on vacation with no schedule or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... well, a vacation sounds lovely, but... That makes sense, right? I'm answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep trouble is related to your body's internal clock?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I mean, I don't really know how the internal clock thing works. \n\nI suppose it could be? Oh, um... I really don't know, to be honest. I haven't actually thought about taking a break or... or changing jobs.

I don't know, they just take me hours sometimes. \n\nI find myself losing the thread of what I'm doing, and I end up rereading the same assumptions over and over again. And then I just... I get so worried I've missed something obvious right before a presentation. I really hate admitting that, because I want to be doing a good job. I mean, everyone gets a little distracted when they're tired, right? It's not like I'm completely incompetent or anything, I just... I have to double-check things more than I used to. Most people probably feel a little sluggish all day when they aren't sleeping, right? That's pretty standard?\n\nDoctor: Do you often go to bed and wake up at the same times every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I'd say I'm pretty consistent. I feel like it's important to maintain a routine so I can be ready for the week ahead and not let things slide. I don't want my family to have to deal with me sleeping in and messing up our weekend plans. I mean, it's good practice to keep a regular sleep schedule, isn't it? I read somewhere that doctors recommend that, so I've really been trying my best to stick to it. I hope that's the right thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling like you're sleeping at the wrong times of the day or night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I just power through it with some water or a quick walk around the office. I'd never want anyone to think I'm slacking off. It's totally normal to get a little sleepy in the afternoon, right? I think a lot of people feel that way, so I just try to handle it as best I can. I hope I'm doing the right thing by just pushing through?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep pattern would change if you were not working?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I don't think it would change much. I feel like if I didn't have that structure, I might just sort of fade into the background, you know? I want to be useful, especially for my family. I wouldn't want to become a burden or just lay around all day. That would be terrible, right? So I'd still make sure I'm up at six, getting things done around the house or running errands. I feel like it's important to maintain a routine so I can be ready for the week ahead and not let things slide. I mean, I really do try to stick to that eleven to six schedule, so I'm not really sleeping during the day at all. \n\nI guess sometimes I might feel a little heavy-eyed in the afternoon, like after lunch, but I never actually fall asleep at my desk or anything. I just power through it with some water or a quick walk around the office. I'd never want anyone to think I'm slacking off. It's totally normal to get a little sleepy in the afternoon, right? I think a lot of people feel that way, so I just try to handle it as best I can. I hope I'm doing the right thing by just pushing through?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep pattern would change if you were not working?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I don't think it would change much. It's better to stay busy and productive, don't you think? I'm pretty sure that's the healthiest way to handle things, even if you're not working.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're more of a night owl, or that your body wants to stay up later and sleep in later than your current schedule allows?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yes, I suppose it is. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I can't handle my job, because I really do love it, and I'm usually very good at managing the pressure.

Most people probably feel a little sluggish all day when they aren't sleeping, right? That's pretty standard?\n\nDoctor: Do you often go to bed and wake up at the same times every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I'd say I'm pretty consistent. I really try to keep a strict schedule, you know? I usually go to bed around eleven and I'm up by six, even on Saturdays and Sundays. \n\nI just... I feel like it's important to maintain a routine so I can be ready for the week ahead and not let things slide. I don't want my family to have to deal with me sleeping in and messing up our weekend plans. I mean, it's good practice to keep a regular sleep schedule, isn't it? I read somewhere that doctors recommend that, so I've really been trying my best to stick to it. I hope that's the right thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling like you're sleeping at the wrong times of the day or night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I really do try to stick to that eleven to six schedule, so I'm not really sleeping during the day at all. \n\nI guess sometimes I might feel a little heavy-eyed in the afternoon, like after lunch, but I never actually fall asleep at my desk or anything. I just power through it with some water or a quick walk around the office. I'd never want anyone to think I'm slacking off. It's totally normal to get a little sleepy in the afternoon, right? I think a lot of people feel that way, so I just try to handle it as best I can. I hope I'm doing the right thing by just pushing through?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep pattern would change if you were not working?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I don't think it would change much. I mean, even if I didn't have the office to go to, I'd still want to keep my routine. \n\nI just... I feel like if I didn't have that structure, I might just sort of fade into the background, you know? I want to be useful, especially for my family. I wouldn't want to become a burden or just lay around all day. That would be terrible, right? So I'd still make sure I'm up at six, getting things done around the house or running errands. It's better to stay busy and productive, don't you think? I'm pretty sure that's the healthiest way to handle things, even if you're not working.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're more of a night owl, or that your body wants to stay up later and sleep in later than your current schedule allows?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I've never really been a night owl, to be honest. I mean, I know some people really thrive late at night, but I just... I don't think my body wants to do that. \n\nI really prefer being up early and getting a head start on the day. It just feels more responsible, you know? Like I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing. If I stayed up late and slept in, I'd just feel so guilty, like I was wasting the day or letting people down. I wouldn't want my husband or kids to think I'm lazy. It's much better to just stick to the schedule and be dependable, right? I think that's what a good mother and wife should do, anyway. I mean, even on weekends or when I have a day off, my eyes just sort of pop open right around six anyway. \n\nIt's like my body is just... trained to do it, I guess. I wouldn't want to sleep in and waste the morning. There's always so much to do—laundry, getting breakfast ready, just making sure the house is in order before everyone else gets going. I feel like if I slept in, I'd just be starting the day off on the wrong foot, you know? It's better to be up and ready to go. That's a good habit to have, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing my part.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still wake up at 6 am if you were on vacation with no schedule or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... well, a vacation sounds lovely, but... I probably wouldn't even make it to 6 am, to be honest. \n\nI... I guess I should just admit this.
